<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Berezinskii–Kosterlitz–Thouless essential singularity for the correlation length of the nearest-neighbor cosine XY model on the square lattice. Let $`m(b)`$ be the mass obtained by taking first the free-box thermodynamic limit and then the separation limit along a coordinate axis. As the inverse temperature <i>b</i> approaches <i>b</i><sub><i>c</i></sub> from the massive side,
> 
> $`\displaystyle \sqrt{b_c-b}\log\frac{1}{m(b)} \longrightarrow A_{\mathrm{XY}},`$
> 
> where $`A_{\mathrm{XY}}`$ is a finite, strictly positive model-specific constant.

- **claim-main-bkt-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For the model <a href="#eq:spin-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:spin-law">[eq:spin-law]</a>, there is a constant $A_{\mathrm{XY}}\in(0,\infty)$ such that $$\label{eq:main-limit} \lim_{b\uparrow b_c}\sqrt{b_c-b}\,\log\xi(b)=A_{\mathrm{XY}}.$$
  - Explanation: As the inverse temperature approaches its critical value from the massive side, the square root of the distance to criticality times the logarithm of the axis correlation length converges to a finite positive constant. This is the essential singularity of the correlation length.
- **claim-finite-comparisons** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-comparisons)): For the finite height laws just described, increasing centered precision decreases centered covariances and real moment generating functions. The covariance in an affine law, also after a real linear tilt, dominates the corresponding centered covariance. A shifted-to-centered partition ratio is at most one and decreases when the same positive precision is added to both laws. The latter monotonicity also holds with a fixed real linear statistic inserted in the exponent of the affine numerator.* *Centered invariant characteristic coefficients are positive and increase with precision. The Hessian of their logarithm at any phase is bounded below by its Hessian at zero. A characteristic test on a freely translated component is invariant when the sum of its coefficients is an integer; a real linear test on such a component must have coefficient sum zero. For a centered real linear test $X$, $$\label{eq:laplace-lower} \log\mathbb Ee^X\ge\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(X).$$ The same centered moment and characteristic comparisons imply that, as $b$ increases, centered covariances and real transforms increase, whereas centered invariant characteristic coefficients decrease.
  - Explanation: On the finite dual height laws, more centered precision lowers covariances and real exponential moments. Affine covariances dominate the centered ones, and characteristic coefficients grow with precision. Consequently, raising the temperature parameter raises centered covariances.
- **claim-reference-cost-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-reference-cost)): Fix a compact interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$. In every centered finite height law above, for $b\in I$ and each fixed $r\ge1$, $$\label{eq:reference-moments} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(X)\le C_I\sum_e w_e^2,\qquad \mathbb E\|X\|^r\le C_{I,r}\Bigl(\sum_e w_e^2\Bigr)^{r/2}.$$ For every fixed $t$, the transform $\mathbb E\exp(tX/(\sum_e w_e^2)^{1/2})$ is bounded uniformly in the graph. More generally, if $\max_e\|tw_e\|\le K$, then $$\label{eq:bounded-flow-mgf} \log\mathbb Ee^{tX}\le C_{I,K}t^2\sum_e w_e^2.$$ Zero tests are interpreted separately.
  - Explanation: Variance, absolute moments and exponential transforms of a linear height test are controlled by its reference cost, uniformly over the graph and over temperatures in a compact interval.
- **claim-factorial-insertions** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-factorial)): In a centered finite height law, including centered primary pins and centered observation penalties, $$\label{eq:factorial-bound} \mathbb E_b(N)_\ell\le(bM)^\ell.$$ If the observation centers are prescribed real values, the unnormalized factorial insertion is bounded by $(bM)^\ell$ times the corresponding zero-observation centered sum.
  - Explanation: Falling factorial moments of the total Poisson count are bounded by the corresponding power of the temperature times the edge count. With prescribed observation centers, the unnormalized insertion obeys the same bound times the zero-observation centered sum.
- **claim-finite-volume-continuity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-perturbation)): Fix $b_0>0$ and a compact set $K$ of complex observation vectors on a fixed finite list of observation shapes. There are constants $c,C>0$ such that, for $\eta\in K$ and $\|b-b_0\|M\le c$, $$\label{eq:finite-tv} \frac{\\|\mu_b^\eta-\mu_{b_0}^\eta\\|_{\rm TV}}{Z_{b_0}^0} \le C\|b-b_0\|M.$$ Here total variation for complex weights means the sum of their absolute values. The constants can be uniform over any prescribed finite family of graph topologies and observation shapes. In particular, each centered normalizer is nonzero on a disk of radius $c/M$ about $b_0$.
  - Explanation: On a finite graph the unnormalized weights, including complex observations, move by an amount proportional to the parameter change times the edge count, and the centered normalizers stay nonzero on a disk of inverse-edge-count radius.
- **claim-temperature-response** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-response)): Let $G$ be a finite connected graph with the centered Bessel height law, and let $f\ne0$ have coefficient sum zero. Set $X=\langle h,f\rangle$ and $G(f)=\langle f,A_G^+f\rangle$. For $b$ in a compact interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$, $$\label{eq:temperature-response} \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_b(X)^2}{4\pi^2b^2G(f)} \le \partial_b\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_b(X) \le C_I\|E\|G(f).$$ In particular, these inequalities hold on every zero-winding periodic torus, with constants independent of its size.
  - Explanation: The temperature derivative of a height variance is bounded below by a quantity built from the variance and the reference cost, and above by the edge count times the reference cost. This gives a strict response on finite graphs, including periodic tori with size-independent constants.
- **claim-analytic-initialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-entry)): For every sufficiently small $\rho>0$, the fixed choices above can be made so that there exist $c_{R_0}>0$ and $s_0<\infty$ with the following property. For every admissible $s\ge s_0$, the ordinary plane and compatible-torus entry activities $K_s(b)$ are Banach analytic on $$\label{eq:entry-parameter-disk} \|b-b_c\|<c_{R_0}s^{-2},$$ and take values in $\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{bulk}}_{L^{j_0},s}$ or its compatible-torus counterpart, with norm at most $\rho$. They are real on real fields when $b$ is real. Their support, symmetry, and local copying identities agree with those at criticality throughout the complex parameter disk. For real parameters in this disk the cutoff partition representation is exact. On a smaller concentric disk, for each fixed $r\ge1$, $$\label{eq:entry-cauchy} \\|\partial_b^rK_s(b)\\|_{L^{j_0},s} \le C_{R_0,r}s^{2r}.$$
  - Explanation: The entry activities of the small-activity map are Banach analytic in the temperature on a complex disk whose radius shrinks like an inverse square of the observation side. They stay small in norm, are real for real temperature, and obey Cauchy derivative bounds.
- **claim-activity-map** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-map)): For all sufficiently large absolute indices $j$, the map on $(t,z,\mathcal R)\in\mathbb C^2\times \mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{bulk}}_{L^j,s}$, with the occupied-support and designated-localization conventions of [[F](#ref-F), Proposition 4.1], preserves the bulk symmetry class and is analytic on a fixed ball $\|t\|+\|z\|+\\|\mathcal R\\|_{L^j,s}<\rho_{\mathrm{map}}$. Its linear part is $$\label{eq:map-triangular} (t,z,\mathcal R)\longmapsto (t+P_j^t\mathcal R,\lambda_jz+P_j^z\mathcal R, \mathsf C_j\mathcal R), \quad \\|\mathsf C_j\\|\le\theta_0<1, \quad \lambda_j=1+O(e^{-cj}),$$ with uniformly bounded rows $P_j^t,P_j^z$. The nonlinear part and its Taylor derivatives have quadratic bounds with uniform constants.* *There are analytic normal coordinates $(u,Z,Q)$, uniform in the entry side, such that the pure Gaussian curve is $(u,0,0)$ and the map fixes $u$ on that curve. With $b_*=2\log L$ and a constant $H>0$, define $$\label{eq:balanced-coordinates} x=b_*u,\qquad y=\sqrt{b_*H}\,Z,\qquad W=(x,y).$$ The coordinate changes obey $$\label{eq:coordinate-comparison} t=u+O(u^2+\\|Q\\|),\qquad z=(1+O(e^{-cj}))Z+O(u^2+\\|Q\\|),\qquad \mathcal R=O(u^2)+Q,$$ with the differentiated analytic bounds of these orders. In a fixed small tube, $$\label{eq:normal-map} \begin{split} W_+&=W+(-y^2,-xy)+\mathcal E_j(W,Q),\\ \|\mathcal E_j(W,Q)\| &\le C\{j^{-2}\|W\|^2+\|W\|^3+(\|W\|+\\|Q\\|)\\|Q\\|\},\\ \\|Q_+\\|&\le\theta\\|Q\\|+C\|W\|^2,\qquad \theta<1. \end{split}$$ The error is an analytic function with the corresponding Taylor bounds on all fixed derivatives.* *On a separately initialized compatible torus with a fixed range buffer, the two singleton coefficients copy the plane coefficients through its stop. The torus remainder has its own contraction and analytic quadratic bound. Its derivative at zero has no singleton forcing. All constants are uniform in the stop, the entry side, and their associated norms.
  - Explanation: The activity map is analytic on a small ball, preserves the bulk symmetry class, has a triangular linear part with a contracting remainder block, and in normal coordinates reduces to a quadratic flow in two scalar coordinates plus controlled errors. A separately initialized torus copies the plane scalars.
- **claim-analytic-torus-observables** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-readouts)): On a fixed small complex ball of terminal activities, the cutoff variance correction and the cutoff phase expectation extend to analytic functions of the activity coordinates. If $\nu_j=\|t_j\|+\|z_j\|+\\|\mathcal R^{\rm tor}_{j\|j}\\|$, they satisfy $$\label{eq:torus-readouts} \begin{split} \widehat\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(X_{n_j,s})-v^0_{n_j,s} &=\kappa_{n_j,s}t_j+O(\\|\mathcal R^{\rm tor}_{j\|j}\\|+\nu_j^2),\\ \widehat\mathbb Ee^{i\bar\eta} &=p_{j,s}z_j+O(\\|\mathcal R^{\rm tor}_{j\|j}\\|+\nu_j^2). \end{split}$$ All fixed activity derivatives have the corresponding Taylor bounds, uniformly in entry and stop. The multipliers are positive, bounded above and below, and $$\label{eq:readout-multipliers} v^0_{n,s}=a\mathfrak g+O(s/n+n^{-2}),\qquad \kappa_{n,s}=a\mathfrak g+O(s/n+n^{-2}).$$ At a common absolute stop, the multipliers $p_{j,s}$ for different entries agree except for their mean-observation-noise factor; their relative difference is $O((s_1/n)^2+(s_2/n)^2)$.
  - Explanation: The cutoff variance correction and cutoff phase expectation of a finite torus are analytic in the terminal activity coordinates. To leading order they are positive multiples of the two scalar coordinates, with multipliers that agree across entries up to a noise factor.
- **claim-cutoff-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff-derivative)): For real $b$ in the interval of Proposition <a href="#prop:entry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:entry">7</a>, and $n\ge sP_0$ with $\log n=o(s^2)$, replacing the original torus expectation by its normalized cutoff expectation changes the variance of $X_{n,s}$ and the phase expectation by at most $$\label{eq:cutoff-derivative-error} Cn^Ce^{-cs^2}.$$ The same bound holds for their first $b$ derivatives and for divided differences between any two real parameters in that interval.
  - Explanation: Replacing the original torus expectation by the normalized cutoff expectation changes the variance and phase expectation, their temperature derivatives and divided differences, by an error that is exponentially small in the square of the cell side.
- **claim-grid-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-grid-comparison)): After dividing the phase expectation by its deterministic mean-noise factor, it is exactly $\mathbb Ee^{i\bar h}$, independently of $s$. After subtracting the independent observation-noise variance and dividing by $\beta_{n,s}^2$, the variance of $X_{n,s}$ differs from $\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}H_n$ by $O((s/n)^4)$. Its first $b$ derivative, and its divided differences over a compact real parameter interval, differ by at most $$\label{eq:grid-derivative-error} Cn^2(s/n)^4.$$ The estimates are uniform for compatible grids with $n/s$ sufficiently large. Cutoff observables incur the additional error in <a href="#eq:cutoff-derivative-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cutoff-derivative-error">[eq:cutoff-derivative-error]</a>.
  - Explanation: After removing the deterministic noise factor, the phase expectation is the microscopic one exactly. The observation variance agrees with the first-cosine variance up to a fourth-order grid correction, and its derivatives and divided differences obey a corresponding bound.
- **claim-critical-histories** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-critical)): The fixed parameters and entry threshold can be chosen so that there is a family $\mathcal H^{(k)}$ at $b=b_c$, with admissible $s_k\asymp2^k$ and entry indices $j_0(k)=O(k)$, having the following properties. For every fixed $0<c<c'<3$, uniformly on $c2^k\le j\le c'2^k$, $$\label{eq:critical-asymptotic} W_j^{(k)}=\frac{(1,1)}j+O\!\left(\frac{\log(2+j)}{j^2}\right), \qquad \\|Q_j^{(k)}\\|+\\|\mathcal R_j^{(k)}\\|=O(j^{-2}).$$ The separately initialized torus remainders also satisfy $O(j^{-2})$ on these stage ranges behind every valid later stop in the same history up to $4\cdot2^k$.* *More generally, for any prescribed sufficiently small tube and any integer bound $J_{\max}(s)=o(s^2)$, the critical plane history at an admissible side $s$ stays in that tube through $J_{\max}(s)$, for all large $s$. Every stage of its separately initialized tori with valid stops below $J_{\max}(s)$ stays in the corresponding small ball. The choices of geometric parameters are independent of $s$ and $J_{\max}$.
  - Explanation: At criticality there are histories whose scalar coordinates follow an inverse-index law with logarithmic correction and whose remainders decay quadratically in the inverse index. The confinement extends to any horizon that is little-o of the squared observation side.
- **claim-nonzero-response** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-response)): For the fixed choices above there is a number $\mathcal P\in(0,\infty)$ such that, with $$V_j^{(k)}= \left.\frac{\partial W_j^{(k)}(b)}{\partial b}\right\|_{b=b_c}, \qquad q_u=(1,-\tfrac12),$$ we have $$\label{eq:traj-response-limit} \sup_{\frac12 2^k\le j\le\frac52 2^k} \left\|\frac{V_j^{(k)}}j-\mathcal Pq_u\right\| \longrightarrow0.$$ The plane transverse remainder and every separately initialized torus remainder, at an index in this range behind a valid stop in the critical history, have temperature derivative bounded by $C\|V_j^{(k)}\|/j+e^{-cj}$.
  - Explanation: The temperature derivative of the critical scalar trajectory, divided by the index, converges uniformly to a strictly positive multiple of the growing eigendirection of the linearized flow. The remainder derivatives are slaved to it.
- **claim-late-entry-critical** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-traj-late-critical)): Let $s\to\infty$ through admissible observation sides, put $J=\lfloor C_*\log s\rfloor$, and let $K(s)\ge J$ satisfy $K(s)=o(s^2)$. For the history with observation side $s$, at $b=b_c$, uniformly for $J/2\le i\le K(s)$, $$\label{eq:traj-late-critical} W_i=\frac{(1,1)}i+O\!\left(\frac{\log i}{i^2}\right), \qquad \\|Q_i\\|+\\|\mathcal R_i\\|=O(i^{-2}).$$ Every separately initialized torus with a valid stop $h\in[i,K(s)]$ also has $\\|\mathcal R_{i\|h}^{\mathrm{tor}}\\|=O(i^{-2})$.
  - Explanation: A critical history entered at a large observation side obeys the canonical critical estimates after a logarithmic initial interval, including the separately initialized tori.
- **claim-temperature-secant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-traj-secant)): For the history with observation side $s$, $$\label{eq:traj-secant-match} \frac{W_J(b_c-\varepsilon)-W_J(b_c)}{-\varepsilon} =J\bigl(\mathcal Pq_u+o(1)\bigr), \qquad \left\\|\frac{Q_J(b_c-\varepsilon)-Q_J(b_c)}{\varepsilon}\right\\|\le C.$$ The same uniform bound holds for the secant of each unbalanced torus remainder at stage $J$ behind a stop of order $\delta^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: At the matching index the finite temperature secant of the scalar coordinates equals the index times the response limit up to a vanishing error, and the remainder secants stay bounded.
- **claim-passage-time** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-passage)): Put $$k_0=\sqrt{3\mathcal P},\qquad T=\frac\pi{k_0}.$$ For $b=b_c-\varepsilon$, use an admissible observation side $s\asymp\varepsilon^{-1/3}$. On every compact subinterval of $(0,T)$, the scalar trajectory satisfies, uniformly for $w=\delta j$ in that subinterval, $$\label{eq:traj-scaled-limit} \frac{x_j(b)}\delta\longrightarrow k_0\cot(k_0w), \qquad \frac{y_j(b)}\delta\longrightarrow k_0\csc(k_0w), \qquad \\|Q_j(b)\\|=O(\delta^2).$$ In particular, for every sequence $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ and $\delta j\to w\in(0,T)$, $$\label{eq:traj-before-variance} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{b,n_j}^{\mathrm{tor}}H_{n_j} \longrightarrow a\mathfrak g.$$ For every sufficiently small fixed $d>0$, there are exit indices $j_e=j_e(\varepsilon,d)$ such that $$\label{eq:traj-exit} \delta j_e\longrightarrow T,\qquad x_{j_e}=-d+O(d^2),\qquad y_{j_e}=O(d),\qquad \\|Q_{j_e}\\|+\\|\mathcal R_{j_e\|j_e}^{\mathrm{tor}}\\|=O(d^2).$$ All states through the exit lie in the small-activity map domain. For some $c_d>0$, $$\label{eq:traj-exit-variance} \limsup_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{b,n_{j_e}}^{\mathrm{tor}}H_{n_{j_e}} \le a\mathfrak g-c_d.$$ The torus law in these assertions is the original periodic Bessel height law in the zero-winding sector, with its common height translation factored out.
  - Explanation: Below criticality the rescaled scalar trajectory converges to the cotangent and cosecant solution on compact subintervals before the pole, and the torus variance stays at its critical Gaussian value there. At exit indices with scaled index tending to the pole time, the variance is bounded strictly below that value.
- **claim-profile-extraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-profile)): Every sequence $b_k\to b_c$ from below has a subsequence and a nonincreasing function $\mathcal A:(0,\infty)\to[0,a]$, where $a=8\pi$, with the following property. At each continuity point $w>0$ of $\mathcal A$, every integer sequence $m_k$ satisfying $\delta_k\log_L m_k\to w$ obeys $$\label{eq:profile-limit} a_{m_k}(b_k)\longrightarrow\mathcal A(w).$$ One may choose $\mathcal A$ to be right continuous.
  - Explanation: Along any sequence of temperatures approaching criticality from below, a subsequence yields a nonincreasing, right-continuous profile of the free-square variance coefficient on logarithmic scales, bounded by the critical coefficient.
- **claim-free-gaussian-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-free)): Let $U_k$, rescaled by $n_k^{-1}$, be free rectangles converging to a fixed nondegenerate axis rectangle $U$. For smooth shifts defined near $\overline U$, $$\label{eq:profile-free-gradient} \log\mathbb E_{U_k,b_k}^{\mathrm{free}}e^{(h,A_{U_k}g_k)} \longrightarrow \frac{a_*}{2}\int_U\|\nabla g\|^2.$$ The joint limits are centered Gaussian and all joint moments converge. For zero-total density smears the limiting covariance is $a_*(-\Delta_{U,N})^{-1}$ on mean-free functions. Face probabilities minus volume probabilities of the same total are also allowed. These conclusions include $a_*=0$.
  - Explanation: On free rectangles at the moving temperature, tests of smooth shifts and zero-total density smears converge jointly to centered Gaussians with the Neumann Green covariance scaled by the profile value, including the zero case.
- **claim-uniform-fractional-mean** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-phase)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:profile-free" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:profile-free">18</a>, suppose $a_*>0$. The fractional mean modulo $2\pi$ converges to uniform measure, independently of the Gaussian observations in that lemma. For every fixed nonzero integer $l$ and every zero-total profile $v_k$ of bounded reference cost, $$\label{eq:profile-phase-zero} \mathbb E\exp\{i(h,lu_{U_k}+v_k)\}\longrightarrow0,$$ where $u_{U_k}$ is uniform probability on $U_k$.
  - Explanation: When the profile value is positive, the fractional height mean modulo the period becomes uniform and asymptotically independent of the Gaussian observations, so characteristic functions with nonzero integer charge vanish.
- **claim-pinned-gaussian-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-pins)): Let $b_k$ lie in a compact interval $J\subset(0,\infty)$, and let $n_k\to\infty$. Suppose that, on every fixed nondegenerate rectangle with mesh $n_k^{-1}$, the free Bessel height laws at $b_k$ have Gaussian Laplace and moment limits for mean-free density smears and energy-approximable weak Laplacian tests, with coefficient $a_*>0$. Suppose also that the fractional mean modulo $2\pi$ converges jointly with those tests to an independent uniform variable.* *Let $U$ be a fixed rectangle and let $p\subset U$ be a nonempty finite union of aligned positive-area boxes. Assume that the resulting pieces have nondegenerate faces and positive-width passages; bounded lattice rounding is allowed. Pin every primary vertex of $p$ to zero. Define $$\mathcal H_p=\{v\in H^1(U):v=0\text{ almost everywhere on }p\}, \qquad \mathcal E_U(v,w)=\int_U\nabla v\cdot\nabla w,$$ and let $G_p$ be the inverse of this form: for a bounded density $f$, $G_pf\in\mathcal H_p$ satisfies $\mathcal E_U(G_pf,v)=\int_Ufv$ for $v\in\mathcal H_p$. For every fixed finite collection of bounded piecewise continuous densities $f_1,\ldots,f_d$, their primary smears converge jointly, with all Laplace transforms and polynomial moments, to the centered Gaussian vector with covariance $$\label{eq:profile-pinned-covariance} a_*\int_U f_iG_pf_j.$$ The densities need not have zero integral.
  - Explanation: Under the stated free-limit and phase assumptions, hard pins on a fixed region give jointly Gaussian limits of density smears whose covariance is the profile value times the grounded Dirichlet Green form, with Laplace transforms and moments converging.
- **claim-gaussian-profile-torus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-gaussian-profile)): Let $w$ be a continuity point of the extracted profile, let $\delta_k\log_Ln_k\to w$, and put $a_*=\mathcal A(w)$. The free rectangular limits are those in Lemma <a href="#lem:profile-free" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:profile-free">18</a>; if $a_*>0$, their fractional means have the conclusion of Lemma <a href="#lem:profile-phase" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:profile-phase">19</a>, and their fixed primary pinned geometries have the Gaussian Laplace and moment limits of Lemma <a href="#lem:profile-pins" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:profile-pins">20</a>. In particular, on a square interior surrounded by a fixed positive-width zero rim, the covariance of bounded piecewise continuous density smears converges to $a_*(-\Delta_D)^{-1}$, with Dirichlet boundary on that square.* *On the zero-winding height torus of side $n_k$, every smooth periodic shift $g$ satisfies $$\label{eq:profile-torus} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}(h,A_{\mathrm{tor}}g_k)\longrightarrow a_*\int_{\mathbb T^2}\|\nabla g\|^2.$$ Thus the area-weighted first cosine observation in <a href="#eq:physical-cosine" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-cosine">[eq:physical-cosine]</a> has limiting variance $a_*\mathfrak g$, where $\mathfrak g=1/(8\pi^2)$.
  - Explanation: At continuity scales the free, fractional-mean and pinned limits hold, and on the zero-winding torus the variance of smooth periodic gradient tests converges to the profile value times the Dirichlet energy. In particular the first-cosine observation has the stated limiting variance.
- **claim-zero-interval-uniform** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-profile-zero-uniform)): Suppose $I=[u,v]\subset(0,\infty)$ consists of continuity points of $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal A=0$ on $I$. Fix a compact set $K\subset\mathbb R^2$ and $\lambda\in\mathbb R$. For a free square of side $m$ write $X_{m,p}=(h,A_{B_m}(p\cdot x/m))$. Then $$\label{eq:profile-zero-uniform} \sup_{\substack{m\in\mathbb N:\ \delta_k\log_Lm\in I\\p\in K}} \left\|\mathbb E_{B_m,b_k}^{\mathrm{free}}e^{\lambda X_{m,p}}-1\right\| \longrightarrow0.$$ The assertion is unchanged by translating the squares or multiplying their sides by factors in a fixed compact subset of $(0,\infty)$, provided their resulting logarithmic scales stay in a fixed zero-profile neighborhood of $I$.
  - Explanation: On an interval of continuity scales where the profile vanishes, exponential moments of bounded affine tests on free squares converge uniformly to one.
- **claim-face-phase-growth** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-ratio)): There is $c>0$, independent of sufficiently large fixed integer $G$, with the following property. If $w$ is a continuity point and $\mathcal A(w)=a_*>0$, then along every sequence $\delta_k\log_Lm_k\to w$, $$\label{eq:profile-ratio} \liminf_k\frac{z_{Gm_k}(b_k)}{z_{m_k}(b_k)} \ge cG^{2-a_* /(4\pi)}.$$ For each fixed such $G$ there is also $c(G)>0$ such that $$\label{eq:profile-ratio-rough} z_{Gm}(b)/z_m(b)\ge c(G)$$ for all sufficiently large $m$ and all $b$ in the fixed compact temperature interval.
  - Explanation: At a continuity scale with positive profile value, the face characteristic coefficient grows under blocking by a power of the block factor, with a prefactor independent of the factor. A cruder positive lower bound holds for each fixed factor.
- **claim-face-phase-subexponential** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-subexponential)): For the fixed buffer $D_0$ and block ratio $L$, $$\label{eq:profile-subexponential} -\log z_{D_0L^j}(b_c)=o(j),\qquad j\to\infty.$$ Moreover $z_m(b)\ge z_m(b_c)$ whenever $b\le b_c$.
  - Explanation: At the critical temperature the negative logarithm of the face phase coefficient grows sublinearly in the scale index, and the coefficient is monotone in temperature, so the lower bound holds for all subcritical temperatures.
- **claim-profile-one-jump** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-profile-step)): The profile extracted in Proposition <a href="#prop:profile" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:profile">17</a> satisfies $$\label{eq:profile-step} \mathcal A(w)=a\quad(0<w<T),\qquad \mathcal A(w)=0\quad(w>T), \qquad T=\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{3\mathcal P}}.$$ In particular all points of these two open intervals are continuity points. No assertion about its value at the single point $T$ is needed.
  - Explanation: The extracted profile equals the critical coefficient before the passage time and vanishes after it, so intermediate values are excluded; no assertion is made at the jump itself.
- **claim-mass-correlation-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mass-correlation-bounds)): For every $b>0$ and $x\in\mathbb Z^2$, $$\label{eq:mass-pointwise} C_b(x)\le e^{-m(b)\\|x\\|_\infty}.$$ If $G$ is a finite subgraph of $\mathbb Z^2$, then $$\label{eq:mass-charge-two} 0\le C^{(2)}_{G,b}(u,v)\le4C_{G,b}(u,v) \le4C_b(v-u).$$
  - Explanation: Infinite-volume correlations decay at least exponentially at the axis mass rate in the supremum norm, and the charge-two correlation on a finite subgraph is bounded by a constant multiple of the charge-one correlation.
- **claim-separated-height-covariance** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mass-loop-covariance)): Fix $d,M,c_1>0$ and a compact $I\subset(0,\infty)$. Let $G_n$ be a free spin rectangle contained in a square of side $c_1n$. On its bounded faces take the dual height law with exterior face height zero. Let $$F_n=\sum_z f_{n,z}h(z),\qquad H_n=\sum_z g_{n,z}h(z),$$ where $\sum_z\|f_{n,z}\|,\sum_z\|g_{n,z}\|\le M$, and the two supports are separated horizontally by at least $dn$. There are constants $c,C>0$, depending only on the displayed fixed parameters, such that, for $b\in I$ and sufficiently large $n$, $$\label{eq:mass-covariance-decay} \|\mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}(F_n,H_n)\|\le Cn^{12}e^{-c n m(b)}+Ce^{-c n^4\log n}.$$
  - Explanation: For horizontally separated height smears on a free rectangle in the loop representation, the covariance is bounded by a polynomially weighted exponential in the product of side length and mass, plus a superexponentially small term.
- **claim-mass-lower-length** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-mass-lower-length)): Along the extracted sequence, $$\label{eq:mass-liminf} \liminf_k\delta_k\log_L\frac1{m(b_k)}\ge T.$$
  - Explanation: Positive pinned height covariance below the passage time forces the scaled logarithmic correlation length to be at least the passage time in the limit inferior.
- **claim-mass-polynomial-decay** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-mass-polynomial)): Fix $w'>T$, positive integers $r_k$ satisfying $\delta_k\log_Lr_k\to w'$, and constants $0<c_0<C_0<\infty$. For every $K>0$, all sufficiently large $k$ satisfy $$\label{eq:mass-polynomial} \sup_{c_0r_k\le\\|x\\|_\infty\le C_0r_k}C_{b_k}(x) \le r_k^{-K}.$$
  - Explanation: At scales above the passage time, spin correlations decay faster than any fixed inverse power of the distance, by inserting fixed circulation tilts over many disjoint annuli where the height coefficient vanishes.
- **claim-finite-size-mass-criterion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mass-finite-size)): Let $H_r=[-r,r]^2\cap\mathbb Z^2$, and put $$S_{r,b}=\sum_{z\in\partial H_r}C_{H_r,b}(0,z).$$ If $S_{r,b}\le1/2$, then $m(b)\ge(\log2)/r$.
  - Explanation: If the boundary correlation sum on a finite square box is small enough, the infinite-volume axis mass is bounded below by a constant over the box radius, via the Lieb–Rivasseau inequality.
- **claim-mass-upper-length** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-mass-upper-length)): Along the extracted sequence, $$\label{eq:mass-limsup} \limsup_k\delta_k\log_L\frac1{m(b_k)}\le T.$$
  - Explanation: Combining the polynomial decay above the passage time with the finite-size criterion bounds the scaled logarithmic correlation length above by the passage time in the limit superior.

More generally, if $\\max_e|tw_e|\\le K$, then $$\\label{eq:bounded-flow-mgf}\n                \\log\\mathbb Ee^{tX}\\le C_{I,K}t^2\\sum_e w_e^2.$$ Zero tests are interpreted separately.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-response",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite connected graph with the centered Bessel height law, and let $f\\ne0$ have coefficient sum zero. Set $X=\\langle h,f\\rangle$ and $G(f)=\\langle f,A_G^+f\\rangle$. For $b$ in a compact interval $I\\subset(0,\\infty)$, $$\\label{eq:temperature-response}\n \\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_b(X)^2}{4\\pi^2b^2G(f)}\n       \\le \\partial_b\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_b(X)\n       \\le C_I|E|G(f).$$ In particular, these inequalities hold on every zero-winding periodic torus, with constants independent of its size.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-response",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-entry",
        "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently small $\\rho>0$, the fixed choices above can be made so that there exist $c_{R_0}>0$ and $s_0<\\infty$ with the following property. For every admissible $s\\ge s_0$, the ordinary plane and compatible-torus entry activities $K_s(b)$ are Banach analytic on $$\\label{eq:entry-parameter-disk}\n                         |b-b_c|<c_{R_0}s^{-2},$$ and take values in $\\mathcal{A}^{\\mathrm{bulk}}_{L^{j_0},s}$ or its compatible-torus counterpart, with norm at most $\\rho$. They are real on real fields when $b$ is real. Their support, symmetry, and local copying identities agree with those at criticality throughout the complex parameter disk. For real parameters in this disk the cutoff partition representation is exact. On a smaller concentric disk, for each fixed $r\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:entry-cauchy}\n                \\|\\partial_b^rK_s(b)\\|_{L^{j_0},s}\n                              \\le C_{R_0,r}s^{2r}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-map",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large absolute indices $j$, the map on $(t,z,\\mathcal R)\\in\\mathbb C^2\\times\n\\mathcal{A}^{\\mathrm{bulk}}_{L^j,s}$, with the occupied-support and designated-localization conventions of [[F](#ref-F), Proposition 4.1], preserves the bulk symmetry class and is analytic on a fixed ball $|t|+|z|+\\|\\mathcal R\\|_{L^j,s}<\\rho_{\\mathrm{map}}$. Its linear part is $$\\label{eq:map-triangular}\n (t,z,\\mathcal R)\\longmapsto\n   (t+P_j^t\\mathcal R,\\lambda_jz+P_j^z\\mathcal R,\n                                                \\mathsf C_j\\mathcal R),\n \\quad \\|\\mathsf C_j\\|\\le\\theta_0<1,\n \\quad \\lambda_j=1+O(e^{-cj}),$$ with uniformly bounded rows $P_j^t,P_j^z$. The nonlinear part and its Taylor derivatives have quadratic bounds with uniform constants.*\n\n\n\n\n*There are analytic normal coordinates $(u,Z,Q)$, uniform in the entry side, such that the pure Gaussian curve is $(u,0,0)$ and the map fixes $u$ on that curve. With $b_*=2\\log L$ and a constant $H>0$, define $$\\label{eq:balanced-coordinates}\n                  x=b_*u,\\qquad y=\\sqrt{b_*H}\\,Z,\\qquad W=(x,y).$$ The coordinate changes obey $$\\label{eq:coordinate-comparison}\n t=u+O(u^2+\\|Q\\|),\\qquad\n z=(1+O(e^{-cj}))Z+O(u^2+\\|Q\\|),\\qquad\n \\mathcal R=O(u^2)+Q,$$ with the differentiated analytic bounds of these orders. In a fixed small tube, $$\\label{eq:normal-map}\n \\begin{split}\n W_+&=W+(-y^2,-xy)+\\mathcal E_j(W,Q),\\\\\n |\\mathcal E_j(W,Q)|\n &\\le C\\{j^{-2}|W|^2+|W|^3+(|W|+\\|Q\\|)\\|Q\\|\\},\\\\\n \\|Q_+\\|&\\le\\theta\\|Q\\|+C|W|^2,\\qquad \\theta<1.\n \\end{split}$$ The error is an analytic function with the corresponding Taylor bounds on all fixed derivatives.*\n\n*On a separately initialized compatible torus with a fixed range buffer, the two singleton coefficients copy the plane coefficients through its stop. The torus remainder has its own contraction and analytic quadratic bound. Its derivative at zero has no singleton forcing. All constants are uniform in the stop, the entry side, and their associated norms.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-map",

Its linear part is $$\\label{eq:map-triangular}\n (t,z,\\mathcal R)\\longmapsto\n   (t+P_j^t\\mathcal R,\\lambda_jz+P_j^z\\mathcal R,\n                                                \\mathsf C_j\\mathcal R),\n \\quad \\|\\mathsf C_j\\|\\le\\theta_0<1,\n \\quad \\lambda_j=1+O(e^{-cj}),$$ with uniformly bounded rows $P_j^t,P_j^z$. The nonlinear part and its Taylor derivatives have quadratic bounds with uniform constants.*\n\n\n\n\n*There are analytic normal coordinates $(u,Z,Q)$, uniform in the entry side, such that the pure Gaussian curve is $(u,0,0)$ and the map fixes $u$ on that curve. With $b_*=2\\log L$ and a constant $H>0$, define $$\\label{eq:balanced-coordinates}\n                  x=b_*u,\\qquad y=\\sqrt{b_*H}\\,Z,\\qquad W=(x,y).$$ The coordinate changes obey $$\\label{eq:coordinate-comparison}\n t=u+O(u^2+\\|Q\\|),\\qquad\n z=(1+O(e^{-cj}))Z+O(u^2+\\|Q\\|),\\qquad\n \\mathcal R=O(u^2)+Q,$$ with the differentiated analytic bounds of these orders. In a fixed small tube, $$\\label{eq:normal-map}\n \\begin{split}\n W_+&=W+(-y^2,-xy)+\\mathcal E_j(W,Q),\\\\\n |\\mathcal E_j(W,Q)|\n &\\le C\\{j^{-2}|W|^2+|W|^3+(|W|+\\|Q\\|)\\|Q\\|\\},\\\\\n \\|Q_+\\|&\\le\\theta\\|Q\\|+C|W|^2,\\qquad \\theta<1.\n \\end{split}$$ The error is an analytic function with the corresponding Taylor bounds on all fixed derivatives.*\n\n*On a separately initialized compatible torus with a fixed range buffer, the two singleton coefficients copy the plane coefficients through its stop. The torus remainder has its own contraction and analytic quadratic bound. Its derivative at zero has no singleton forcing.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-readouts",
        "exact_quote": "On a fixed small complex ball of terminal activities, the cutoff variance correction and the cutoff phase expectation extend to analytic functions of the activity coordinates. If $\\nu_j=|t_j|+|z_j|+\\|\\mathcal R^{\\rm tor}_{j|j}\\|$, they satisfy $$\\label{eq:torus-readouts}\n \\begin{split}\n \\widehat\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X_{n_j,s})-v^0_{n_j,s}\n      &=\\kappa_{n_j,s}t_j+O(\\|\\mathcal R^{\\rm tor}_{j|j}\\|+\\nu_j^2),\\\\\n \\widehat\\mathbb Ee^{i\\bar\\eta}\n      &=p_{j,s}z_j+O(\\|\\mathcal R^{\\rm tor}_{j|j}\\|+\\nu_j^2).\n \\end{split}$$ All fixed activity derivatives have the corresponding Taylor bounds, uniformly in entry and stop. The multipliers are positive, bounded above and below, and $$\\label{eq:readout-multipliers}\n v^0_{n,s}=a\\mathfrak g+O(s/n+n^{-2}),\\qquad\n \\kappa_{n,s}=a\\mathfrak g+O(s/n+n^{-2}).$$ At a common absolute stop, the multipliers $p_{j,s}$ for different entries agree except for their mean-observation-noise factor; their relative difference is $O((s_1/n)^2+(s_2/n)^2)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-readouts",
          "exact_quote": "On a fixed small complex ball of terminal activities, the cutoff variance correction and the cutoff phase expectation extend to analytic functions of the activity coordinates. The multipliers are positive, bounded above and below, and $$\\label{eq:readout-multipliers}\n v^0_{n,s}=a\\mathfrak g+O(s/n+n^{-2}),\\qquad\n \\kappa_{n,s}=a\\mathfrak g+O(s/n+n^{-2}).$$ At a common absolute stop, the multipliers $p_{j,s}$ for different entries agree except for their mean-observation-noise factor; their relative difference is $O((s_1/n)^2+(s_2/n)^2)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.4"

      "exact_statement": "On a fixed small complex ball of terminal activities, the cutoff variance correction and the cutoff phase expectation extend to analytic functions of the activity coordinates. If $\\nu_j=|t_j|+|z_j|+\\|\\mathcal R^{\\rm tor}_{j|j}\\|$, they satisfy $$\\label{eq:torus-readouts}\n \\begin{split}\n \\widehat\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(X_{n_j,s})-v^0_{n_j,s}\n      &=\\kappa_{n_j,s}t_j+O(\\|\\mathcal R^{\\rm tor}_{j|j}\\|+\\nu_j^2),\\\\\n \\widehat\\mathbb Ee^{i\\bar\\eta}\n      &=p_{j,s}z_j+O(\\|\\mathcal R^{\\rm tor}_{j|j}\\|+\\nu_j^2).\n \\end{split}$$ All fixed activity derivatives have the corresponding Taylor bounds, uniformly in entry and stop. The multipliers are positive, bounded above and below, and $$\\label{eq:readout-multipliers}\n v^0_{n,s}=a\\mathfrak g+O(s/n+n^{-2}),\\qquad\n \\kappa_{n,s}=a\\mathfrak g+O(s/n+n^{-2}).$$ At a common absolute stop, the multipliers $p_{j,s}$ for different entries agree except for their mean-observation-noise factor; their relative difference is $O((s_1/n)^2+(s_2/n)^2)$.",
      "explanation": "The cutoff variance correction and cutoff phase expectation of a finite torus are analytic in the terminal activity coordinates. To leading order they are positive multiples of the two scalar coordinates, with multipliers that agree across entries up to a noise factor.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-readouts",
          "exact_quote": "On a fixed small complex ball of terminal activities,",
          "source_id": "PM-216.4"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-analytic-torus-observables",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prop-readouts"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cutoff-derivative",
        "exact_quote": "For real $b$ in the interval of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:entry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:entry\">7</a>, and $n\\ge sP_0$ with $\\log n=o(s^2)$, replacing the original torus expectation by its normalized cutoff expectation changes the variance of $X_{n,s}$ and the phase expectation by at most $$\\label{eq:cutoff-derivative-error}\n                                Cn^Ce^{-cs^2}.$$ The same bound holds for their first $b$ derivatives and for divided differences between any two real parameters in that interval.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cutoff-derivative",

          "source_id": "PM-216.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For real $b$ in the interval of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:entry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:entry\">7</a>, and $n\\ge sP_0$ with $\\log n=o(s^2)$, replacing the original torus expectation by its normalized cutoff expectation changes the variance of $X_{n,s}$ and the phase expectation by at most $$\\label{eq:cutoff-derivative-error}\n                                Cn^Ce^{-cs^2}.$$ The same bound holds for their first $b$ derivatives and for divided differences between any two real parameters in that interval.",
      "explanation": "Replacing the original torus expectation by the normalized cutoff expectation changes the variance and phase expectation, their temperature derivatives and divided differences, by an error that is exponentially small in the square of the cell side.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-grid-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "After dividing the phase expectation by its deterministic mean-noise factor, it is exactly $\\mathbb Ee^{i\\bar h}$, independently of $s$. After subtracting the independent observation-noise variance and dividing by $\\beta_{n,s}^2$, the variance of $X_{n,s}$ differs from $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}H_n$ by $O((s/n)^4)$. Its first $b$ derivative, and its divided differences over a compact real parameter interval, differ by at most $$\\label{eq:grid-derivative-error}\n                                  Cn^2(s/n)^4.$$ The estimates are uniform for compatible grids with $n/s$ sufficiently large. Cutoff observables incur the additional error in <a href=\"#eq:cutoff-derivative-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cutoff-derivative-error\">[eq:cutoff-derivative-error]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-critical",
        "exact_quote": "The fixed parameters and entry threshold can be chosen so that there is a family $\\mathcal H^{(k)}$ at $b=b_c$, with admissible $s_k\\asymp2^k$ and entry indices $j_0(k)=O(k)$, having the following properties. For every fixed $0<c<c'<3$, uniformly on $c2^k\\le j\\le c'2^k$, $$\\label{eq:critical-asymptotic}\n W_j^{(k)}=\\frac{(1,1)}j+O\\!\\left(\\frac{\\log(2+j)}{j^2}\\right),\n \\qquad\n \\|Q_j^{(k)}\\|+\\|\\mathcal R_j^{(k)}\\|=O(j^{-2}).$$ The separately initialized torus remainders also satisfy $O(j^{-2})$ on these stage ranges behind every valid later stop in the same history up to $4\\cdot2^k$.*\n\n*More generally, for any prescribed sufficiently small tube and any integer bound $J_{\\max}(s)=o(s^2)$, the critical plane history at an admissible side $s$ stays in that tube through $J_{\\max}(s)$, for all large $s$. Every stage of its separately initialized tori with valid stops below $J_{\\max}(s)$ stays in the corresponding small ball. The choices of geometric parameters are independent of $s$ and $J_{\\max}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-critical",

      "exact_statement": "The fixed parameters and entry threshold can be chosen so that there is a family $\\mathcal H^{(k)}$ at $b=b_c$, with admissible $s_k\\asymp2^k$ and entry indices $j_0(k)=O(k)$, having the following properties.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-response",
        "exact_quote": "For the fixed choices above there is a number $\\mathcal P\\in(0,\\infty)$ such that, with $$V_j^{(k)}=\n \\left.\\frac{\\partial W_j^{(k)}(b)}{\\partial b}\\right|_{b=b_c},\n \\qquad q_u=(1,-\\tfrac12),$$ we have $$\\label{eq:traj-response-limit}\n \\sup_{\\frac12 2^k\\le j\\le\\frac52 2^k}\n \\left|\\frac{V_j^{(k)}}j-\\mathcal Pq_u\\right|\n \\longrightarrow0.$$ The plane transverse remainder and every separately initialized torus remainder, at an index in this range behind a valid stop in the critical history, have temperature derivative bounded by $C|V_j^{(k)}|/j+e^{-cj}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-response",

          "source_id": "PM-216.4"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-traj-late-critical",
        "exact_quote": "Let $s\\to\\infty$ through admissible observation sides, put $J=\\lfloor C_*\\log s\\rfloor$, and let $K(s)\\ge J$ satisfy $K(s)=o(s^2)$. For the history with observation side $s$, at $b=b_c$, uniformly for $J/2\\le i\\le K(s)$, $$\\label{eq:traj-late-critical}\n W_i=\\frac{(1,1)}i+O\\!\\left(\\frac{\\log i}{i^2}\\right),\n \\qquad\n \\|Q_i\\|+\\|\\mathcal R_i\\|=O(i^{-2}).$$ Every separately initialized torus with a valid stop $h\\in[i,K(s)]$ also has $\\|\\mathcal R_{i|h}^{\\mathrm{tor}}\\|=O(i^{-2})$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "exact_statement": "Let $s\\to\\infty$ through admissible observation sides, put $J=\\lfloor C_*\\log s\\rfloor$, and let $K(s)\\ge J$ satisfy $K(s)=o(s^2)$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-traj-secant",
        "exact_quote": "For the history with observation side $s$, $$\\label{eq:traj-secant-match}\n \\frac{W_J(b_c-\\varepsilon)-W_J(b_c)}{-\\varepsilon}\n      =J\\bigl(\\mathcal Pq_u+o(1)\\bigr),\n \\qquad\n \\left\\|\\frac{Q_J(b_c-\\varepsilon)-Q_J(b_c)}{\\varepsilon}\\right\\|\\le C.$$ The same uniform bound holds for the secant of each unbalanced torus remainder at stage $J$ behind a stop of order $\\delta^{-1}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-traj-secant",
          "exact_quote": "For the history with observation side $s$, $$\\label{eq:traj-secant-match}\n \\frac{W_J(b_c-\\varepsilon)-W_J(b_c)}{-\\varepsilon}\n      =J\\bigl(\\mathcal Pq_u+o(1)\\bigr),\n \\qquad\n \\left\\|\\frac{Q_J(b_c-\\varepsilon)-Q_J(b_c)}{\\varepsilon}\\right\\|\\le C.$$ The same uniform bound holds for the secant of each unbalanced torus remainder at stage $J$ behind a stop of order $\\delta^{-1}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the history with observation side $s$, $$\\label{eq:traj-secant-match}\n \\frac{W_J(b_c-\\varepsilon)-W_J(b_c)}{-\\varepsilon}\n      =J\\bigl(\\mathcal Pq_u+o(1)\\bigr),\n \\qquad\n \\left\\|\\frac{Q_J(b_c-\\varepsilon)-Q_J(b_c)}{\\varepsilon}\\right\\|\\le C.$$ The same uniform bound holds for the secant of each unbalanced torus remainder at stage $J$ behind a stop of order $\\delta^{-1}$.",
      "explanation": "At the matching index the finite temperature secant of the scalar coordinates equals the index times the response limit up to a vanishing error, and the remainder secants stay bounded.",

      "id": "claim-temperature-secant",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-passage",
        "exact_quote": "Put $$k_0=\\sqrt{3\\mathcal P},\\qquad T=\\frac\\pi{k_0}.$$ For $b=b_c-\\varepsilon$, use an admissible observation side $s\\asymp\\varepsilon^{-1/3}$. On every compact subinterval of $(0,T)$, the scalar trajectory satisfies, uniformly for $w=\\delta j$ in that subinterval, $$\\label{eq:traj-scaled-limit}\n \\frac{x_j(b)}\\delta\\longrightarrow k_0\\cot(k_0w),\n \\qquad\n \\frac{y_j(b)}\\delta\\longrightarrow k_0\\csc(k_0w),\n \\qquad \\|Q_j(b)\\|=O(\\delta^2).$$ In particular, for every sequence $\\varepsilon\\downarrow0$ and $\\delta j\\to w\\in(0,T)$, $$\\label{eq:traj-before-variance}\n                \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_{b,n_j}^{\\mathrm{tor}}H_{n_j}\n                       \\longrightarrow a\\mathfrak g.$$ For every sufficiently small fixed $d>0$, there are exit indices $j_e=j_e(\\varepsilon,d)$ such that $$\\label{eq:traj-exit}\n \\delta j_e\\longrightarrow T,\\qquad\n x_{j_e}=-d+O(d^2),\\qquad y_{j_e}=O(d),\\qquad\n \\|Q_{j_e}\\|+\\|\\mathcal R_{j_e|j_e}^{\\mathrm{tor}}\\|=O(d^2).$$ All states through the exit lie in the small-activity map domain. For some $c_d>0$, $$\\label{eq:traj-exit-variance}\n \\limsup_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_{b,n_{j_e}}^{\\mathrm{tor}}H_{n_{j_e}}\n                 \\le a\\mathfrak g-c_d.$$ The torus law in these assertions is the original periodic Bessel height law in the zero-winding sector, with its common height translation factored out.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-passage",
          "exact_quote": "Put $$k_0=\\sqrt{3\\mathcal P},\\qquad T=\\frac\\pi{k_0}.$$ For $b=b_c-\\varepsilon$, use an admissible observation side $s\\asymp\\varepsilon^{-1/3}$. For some $c_d>0$, $$\\label{eq:traj-exit-variance}\n \\limsup_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_{b,n_{j_e}}^{\\mathrm{tor}}H_{n_{j_e}}\n                 \\le a\\mathfrak g-c_d.$$ The torus law in these assertions is the original periodic Bessel height law in the zero-winding sector, with its common height translation factored out.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Put $$k_0=\\sqrt{3\\mathcal P},\\qquad T=\\frac\\pi{k_0}.$$ For $b=b_c-\\varepsilon$, use an admissible observation side $s\\asymp\\varepsilon^{-1/3}$. On every compact subinterval of $(0,T)$, the scalar trajectory satisfies, uniformly for $w=\\delta j$ in that subinterval, $$\\label{eq:traj-scaled-limit}\n \\frac{x_j(b)}\\delta\\longrightarrow k_0\\cot(k_0w),\n \\qquad\n \\frac{y_j(b)}\\delta\\longrightarrow k_0\\csc(k_0w),\n \\qquad \\|Q_j(b)\\|=O(\\delta^2).$$ In particular, for every sequence $\\varepsilon\\downarrow0$ and $\\delta j\\to w\\in(0,T)$, $$\\label{eq:traj-before-variance}\n                \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_{b,n_j}^{\\mathrm{tor}}H_{n_j}\n                       \\longrightarrow a\\mathfrak g.$$ For every sufficiently small fixed $d>0$, there are exit indices $j_e=j_e(\\varepsilon,d)$ such that $$\\label{eq:traj-exit}\n \\delta j_e\\longrightarrow T,\\qquad\n x_{j_e}=-d+O(d^2),\\qquad y_{j_e}=O(d),\\qquad\n \\|Q_{j_e}\\|+\\|\\mathcal R_{j_e|j_e}^{\\mathrm{tor}}\\|=O(d^2).$$ All states through the exit lie in the small-activity map domain. For some $c_d>0$, $$\\label{eq:traj-exit-variance}\n \\limsup_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_{b,n_{j_e}}^{\\mathrm{tor}}H_{n_{j_e}}\n                 \\le a\\mathfrak g-c_d.$$ The torus law in these assertions is the original periodic Bessel height law in the zero-winding sector, with its common height translation factored out.",
      "explanation": "Below criticality the rescaled scalar trajectory converges to the cotangent and cosecant solution on compact subintervals before the pole, and the torus variance stays at its critical Gaussian value there.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-profile-free",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U_k$, rescaled by $n_k^{-1}$, be free rectangles converging to a fixed nondegenerate axis rectangle $U$. For smooth shifts defined near $\\overline U$, $$\\label{eq:profile-free-gradient}\n \\log\\mathbb E_{U_k,b_k}^{\\mathrm{free}}e^{(h,A_{U_k}g_k)}\n       \\longrightarrow \\frac{a_*}{2}\\int_U|\\nabla g|^2.$$ The joint limits are centered Gaussian and all joint moments converge. For zero-total density smears the limiting covariance is $a_*(-\\Delta_{U,N})^{-1}$ on mean-free functions. Face probabilities minus volume probabilities of the same total are also allowed.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-profile-phase",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:profile-free\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:profile-free\">18</a>, suppose $a_*>0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-profile-pins",
        "exact_quote": "Let $b_k$ lie in a compact interval $J\\subset(0,\\infty)$, and let $n_k\\to\\infty$. Suppose that, on every fixed nondegenerate rectangle with mesh $n_k^{-1}$, the free Bessel height laws at $b_k$ have Gaussian Laplace and moment limits for mean-free density smears and energy-approximable weak Laplacian tests, with coefficient $a_*>0$. Suppose also that the fractional mean modulo $2\\pi$ converges jointly with those tests to an independent uniform variable.*\n\n\n*Let $U$ be a fixed rectangle and let $p\\subset U$ be a nonempty finite union of aligned positive-area boxes. Assume that the resulting pieces have nondegenerate faces and positive-width passages; bounded lattice rounding is allowed. Pin every primary vertex of $p$ to zero. Define $$\\mathcal H_p=\\{v\\in H^1(U):v=0\\text{ almost everywhere on }p\\},\n \\qquad \\mathcal E_U(v,w)=\\int_U\\nabla v\\cdot\\nabla w,$$ and let $G_p$ be the inverse of this form: for a bounded density $f$, $G_pf\\in\\mathcal H_p$ satisfies $\\mathcal E_U(G_pf,v)=\\int_Ufv$ for $v\\in\\mathcal H_p$. For every fixed finite collection of bounded piecewise continuous densities $f_1,\\ldots,f_d$, their primary smears converge jointly, with all Laplace transforms and polynomial moments, to the centered Gaussian vector with covariance $$\\label{eq:profile-pinned-covariance}\n                  a_*\\int_U f_iG_pf_j.$$ The densities need not have zero integral.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-gaussian-profile",
        "exact_quote": "Let $w$ be a continuity point of the extracted profile, let $\\delta_k\\log_Ln_k\\to w$, and put $a_*=\\mathcal A(w)$. The free rectangular limits are those in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:profile-free\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:profile-free\">18</a>; if $a_*>0$, their fractional means have the conclusion of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:profile-phase\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:profile-phase\">19</a>, and their fixed primary pinned geometries have the Gaussian Laplace and moment limits of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:profile-pins\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:profile-pins\">20</a>. In particular, on a square interior surrounded by a fixed positive-width zero rim, the covariance of bounded piecewise continuous density smears converges to $a_*(-\\Delta_D)^{-1}$, with Dirichlet boundary on that square.*\n\n\n*On the zero-winding height torus of side $n_k$, every smooth periodic shift $g$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:profile-torus}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(h,A_{\\mathrm{tor}}g_k)\\longrightarrow\n                         a_*\\int_{\\mathbb T^2}|\\nabla g|^2.$$ Thus the area-weighted first cosine observation in <a href=\"#eq:physical-cosine\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:physical-cosine\">[eq:physical-cosine]</a> has limiting variance $a_*\\mathfrak g$, where $\\mathfrak g=1/(8\\pi^2)$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-profile-zero-uniform",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $I=[u,v]\\subset(0,\\infty)$ consists of continuity points of $\\mathcal A$ and $\\mathcal A=0$ on $I$. Fix a compact set $K\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ and $\\lambda\\in\\mathbb R$. For a free square of side $m$ write $X_{m,p}=(h,A_{B_m}(p\\cdot x/m))$. Then $$\\label{eq:profile-zero-uniform}\n \\sup_{\\substack{m\\in\\mathbb N:\\ \\delta_k\\log_Lm\\in I\\\\p\\in K}}\n \\left|\\mathbb E_{B_m,b_k}^{\\mathrm{free}}e^{\\lambda X_{m,p}}-1\\right|\n                                      \\longrightarrow0.$$ The assertion is unchanged by translating the squares or multiplying their sides by factors in a fixed compact subset of $(0,\\infty)$, provided their resulting logarithmic scales stay in a fixed zero-profile neighborhood of $I$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-profile-ratio",
        "exact_quote": "There is $c>0$, independent of sufficiently large fixed integer $G$, with the following property.         "source_id": "PM-216.4"

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **axis correlation length** ([source](#eq:mass-definition)): The axis correlation length in the massive phase is $\xi(b)=m(b)^{-1}$.
- **reference cost** ([source](#pm-h-6)): $$ The full chain therefore converges to <a href="#eq:bessel-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bessel-weight">[eq:bessel-weight]</a>, including all fixed exponential moments. Matching endpoints, cycle constraints, period constraints, and the additional observations are positive quadratic forms or limits of such forms on the enlarged lattice. The finite lattice-Gaussian comparison inequalities apply there. Increasing $b$ decreases the bond precision $2\log(2N/b)$, proving the required monotonicities before passage to the limit. A spanning-forest parametrization and full-chain exponential moments dominate the sums on each fixed primary graph. This justifies the limit, including fixed polynomial insertions and real tilts. Confined constants are dominated by their observation penalties; unconfined constants are factored out. ◻ </details> If $X=\sum_e w_e\nabla_eh$, its *reference cost* is $\sum_e w_e^2$, minimized over flows representing the same test.
- **scaled logarithmic length** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Put $\delta=\sqrt{b_c-b}$ and let $w=\delta j$ be the scaled logarithmic length.
- **reference field scaling and period** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Fix $\alpha=\sqrt a$ and $\omega=2\pi/\alpha$.
- **edge orientation and gradient** ([source](#eq:bessel-weight)): For a finite primary height graph $G=(V,E)$, orient its edges once and write $\nabla_e h=h_{e^+}-h_{e^-}$.

| q-entry-disk-radius | distance of b from b_c on the analyticity disk | < | $c_{R_0}s^{-2}$ | Admissible observation sides at least the threshold. |
| q-flow-scale | flow scale b_* | = | $2\log L$ | Block ratio L. |
| q-profile-below-passage | height coefficient profile | = | $8\pi$ | Scaled length w strictly between zero and T. |
| q-charge-two-factor | charge-two correlation | ≤ | $4C_{G,b}(u,v)$ | All vertices u, v of G. |
| q-finite-size-mass-bound | axis mass m(b) | ≥ | $(\log2)/r$ | Boundary correlation sum at most one half. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-finite-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparisons) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparisons): Approximate each Bessel edge by a chain of lattice-Gaussian bonds and apply the lattice-Gaussian comparison inequalities, then pass to the limit. -> monotonicity of covariances and transforms in precision and temperature
- **step-reference-cost** [claim](#stmt-lem-reference-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reference-cost): Release constraints in the full-chain construction and bound the transform by independent whole-chain transforms. -> uniform moment and transform bounds in terms of reference cost
- **step-factorial-insertions** [claim](#stmt-lem-factorial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-factorial): Expand the falling factorial over ordered choices of Poisson units, remove them, and compare the shifted sum with the centered one. -> factorial moment bound
- **step-finite-continuity** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-perturbation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-perturbation): Write the parameter likelihood in the augmented variables, expand in falling factorials and sum with the factorial bound. -> total variation continuity and nonvanishing normalizers
- **step-temperature-response** [claim](#stmt-lem-response) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-response): Differentiate the augmented law with the score insertion, remove one Poisson unit to get an exact identity, then apply integration by parts and Cauchy–Schwarz. -> two-sided bound on the temperature derivative of a variance
- **step-analytic-entry** [claim](#stmt-prop-entry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-entry): Prove small pattern bounds for real parameters, a coarse majorant on a complex disk, then recover smallness with the two-constants theorem and sum the patterns. -> analytic entry activities with Cauchy derivative bounds
- **step-activity-map** [claim](#stmt-prop-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-map): Build balanced linear coordinates and normal coordinates from the imported exact map and its parity estimates. -> normal form of the map with contracting remainder
- **step-torus-readouts** [claim](#stmt-prop-readouts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-readouts): Complete the Gaussian source before entry averaging and read off the singleton contributions on the fixed terminal grid. -> analytic variance and phase readouts
- **step-cutoff-error** [claim](#stmt-lem-cutoff-derivative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cutoff-derivative): Bound the face-difference tail by the cell-flow argument and differentiate with the Poisson score and Hölder. -> exponentially small cutoff error with derivatives
- **step-grid-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-grid-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-grid-comparison): Project the observation profile onto the first cosine, bound alias modes by reference cost and differentiate with the response bound. -> grid comparison of phase and variance
- **step-critical-histories** [claim](#stmt-prop-critical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-critical): Use the imported critical histories, then extend them by a first-exit contradiction argument with contraction of remainders. -> critical histories in a small tube
- **step-nonzero-response** [claim](#stmt-prop-response) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-response): Differentiate the map, slave remainder derivatives to the scalars, compare entries through physical readouts and run an epoch recursion with an invariant cone; positivity follows from the finite-volume lower bound. -> positive response constant
- **step-late-critical** [claim](#stmt-lem-traj-late-critical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-traj-late-critical): Compare with a canonical history through the readouts and iterate the remainder contraction. -> critical estimates after an initial interval
- **step-secant** [claim](#stmt-lem-traj-secant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-traj-secant): Bound the crude proximity, slave the remainder secants, then compare with a canonical history using Cauchy estimates on a disk. -> matching of the temperature secant
- **step-passage-time** [claim](#stmt-prop-passage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-passage): Close a bootstrap on the early perturbation, take the continuum limit to the explicit flow, then trap the outgoing part in a cone with reciprocal summation. -> passage time and exit variance bound
- **step-profile-extraction** [claim](#stmt-prop-profile) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-profile): Use a diagonal subsequence over rational scales, order the limits by cutting comparisons and regularize to the right. -> nonincreasing profile
- **step-free-gaussian** [claim](#stmt-lem-profile-free) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profile-free): Tile for the affine upper bound, enclose for saturation, use reflection and hinge transport for general shifts, then Laplace bounds. -> free Gaussian limits
- **step-fractional-mean** [claim](#stmt-lem-profile-phase) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profile-phase): Soft joining of squares with a replica identity and a sector sum, contradicting the variance of derivatives. -> uniform fractional mean
- **step-pinned-limits** [claim](#stmt-lem-profile-pins) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profile-pins): Band confinement, cable exploration estimates showing vanishing reach, and grounded approximation of density smears. -> pinned Gaussian limits
- **step-gaussian-profile** [claim](#stmt-prop-gaussian-profile) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gaussian-profile): Cut the torus into rectangles for the upper bound and use pinned collars and a partition of unity for the lower bound. -> torus variance limit
- **step-zero-uniform** [claim](#stmt-cor-profile-zero-uniform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-profile-zero-uniform): Argue by contradiction with a subsequence and use uniform integrability of the exponentials. -> uniform transforms near one
- **step-face-phase-ratio** [claim](#stmt-lem-profile-ratio) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profile-ratio): Soft joining of blocked cells, Poisson summation into sectors and a radial flow cost estimate. -> relative growth of the face phase
- **step-face-phase-subexponential** [claim](#stmt-lem-profile-subexponential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profile-subexponential): Iterate the critical-temperature growth bound on residue classes and let the stride grow. -> sublinear decay exponent of the face phase
- **step-profile-step** [claim](#stmt-prop-profile-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-profile-step): Exclude intermediate values by integrating the relative growth with Fatou's lemma against a telescoping bound, then fix values with the torus variance on each side of the passage time. -> step profile
- **step-mass-correlation-bounds** [claim](#stmt-lem-mass-correlation-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mass-correlation-bounds): Use Ginibre inequalities for supermultiplicativity and a sign argument for the charge-two bound. -> pointwise decay and charge-two bound
- **step-loop-covariance** [claim](#stmt-lem-mass-loop-covariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mass-loop-covariance): Condition on the paired loop geometry, require a large loop and bound its probability by the loop connection estimate and a Poisson tail. -> separated covariance bound
- **step-mass-lower-length** [claim](#stmt-prop-mass-lower-length) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-mass-lower-length): Argue by contradiction: the covariance bound forces vanishing pinned covariance, contradicting its positive limit. -> lower bound on the scaled correlation length
- **step-mass-polynomial** [claim](#stmt-prop-mass-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-mass-polynomial): Insert a fixed circulation tilt on many disjoint annuli, release constraints into cells and use the zero-profile transforms. -> superpolynomial decay
- **step-finite-size-criterion** [claim](#stmt-lem-mass-finite-size) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mass-finite-size): Iterate the Lieb–Rivasseau inequality in translated boxes. -> mass lower bound
- **step-mass-upper-length** [claim](#stmt-prop-mass-upper-length) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-mass-upper-length): Apply the polynomial decay to the boundary sum and then the finite-size criterion. -> upper bound on the scaled correlation length
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-mass-upper-length-2): Combine the two bounds on a subsequence and remove the extraction by contradiction. -> limit of the scaled logarithm of the correlation length

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-limit**: As b increases to b_c from the massive side, the product of the square root of b_c minus b and the logarithm of the axis correlation length tends to a finite, strictly positive constant A_XY.
- **inv-profile-step**: Along a subsequence of subcritical temperatures, the height coefficient profile equals the critical value below the passage scale T and vanishes above it.
- **inv-finite-size-mass**: If the boundary correlation sum on a finite square box is at most one half, then the mass is bounded below by the logarithm of two divided by the box radius.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-universal-amplitude**: Reuse the amplitude A_XY for a different two-dimensional model or a different normalization of the interaction. -> The amplitude is tied to the cosine interaction and to the scaling of b, so a transplanted value is not justified by the theorem.
- **anti-low-temperature-side**: Apply the correlation length asymptotic at or beyond the critical inverse temperature. -> There the mass vanishes, so the correlation length is not defined by the massive-phase formula and the limit is only from below.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main theorem and its supporting propositions are asymptotic limit statements obtained by compactness, subsequence extraction and analytic estimates on infinite-volume or diverging scales; the amplitude is expressed through an abstract response constant that is not given by any finite procedure. The finite-size mass criterion needs exact correlation sums that the paper gives no finite algorithm to compute, so there is no finite constructive object to execute and test.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper is an asymptotic existence theorem about the correlation length of a lattice spin model. It specifies no interface, procedure or data format that a program could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing here is a real-time or interactive simulation rule. The model is an equilibrium Gibbs measure with free boundary, and the result is a limit statement, not a stepping scheme. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No checker, converter or analysis utility is defined. The only finite criterion, a boundary-sum test on a square box, is a lemma inside a limit proof and is not a packaged tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof is analytic: compactness, subsequence extraction and renormalization-flow estimates with unspecified constants. It gives no finite procedure with a running time or an output that is computed. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No representation, index or container is proposed. The integer-height and loop descriptions are proof devices for correlation bounds, not storage layouts. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no arithmetic kernel, precision budget or parallel schedule that could be mapped to hardware, and its constants are not made explicit. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Lattice-Gaussian inequalities appear only as comparison tools for correlation functions. There is no hardness assumption, primitive or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, data set, loss or generalisation statement occurs. The random fields are physical Gibbs laws and their limits. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no objective to optimise over a feasible set. The flow and cost quantities that appear are reference-cost bounds used to control variances. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No protocol, replication, consensus or communication model is present. Spatial decoupling statements concern correlations of a spin measure, not message passing. |
| other (statistical-physics benchmark validation) | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A numerical study of the nearest-neighbor cosine model on the square lattice could use the stated scaling as a qualitative check that the correlation length grows faster than any power of the distance to criticality. This is only an adaptation: the result is non-constructive and gives no value of the amplitude. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-massive-side-only** (hypothesis): The limit is taken with the inverse temperature approaching its critical value from the massive side, where the mass is positive and finite. At and beyond the critical value the mass vanishes, so the correlation length from the massive-phase formula is not defined. Blocked use: ['Applying the correlation length asymptotic at or above the critical inverse temperature', 'Extrapolating the formula into the algebraic-decay phase']
- **limit-amplitude-model-specific** (formal_scope): The amplitude is stated to depend on the normalization of the cosine interaction and is not given in closed form. The theorem only asserts that it is finite and strictly positive. Blocked use: ['Reusing the amplitude for another Hamiltonian or another temperature normalization', 'Treating the amplitude as a computable number in software']
- **limit-no-value-at-jump** (edge_case): The height coefficient profile is determined only on the two open intervals on either side of the passage scale. Nothing is asserted at the single transition point. Blocked use: ['Reading off a value of the profile at the passage scale', 'Using the profile as a continuous function across the jump']
- **limit-subsequence-extraction** (nonconstructive): The profile and the passage-scale identification are obtained along subsequences chosen by compactness. The full limit is recovered only by a contradiction argument, so no explicit rule produces the subsequence. Blocked use: ['Implementing the subsequence extraction as an algorithm', 'Claiming a rate of convergence for the scaled correlation length']
- **limit-imported-companion-inputs** (formal_scope): The critical height coefficient, the finite comparisons and the analytic activity map are taken from two companion works at their stated scope. The conclusions here depend on those results holding in exactly that scope. Blocked use: ['Treating the argument as self-contained without the companion results', 'Transferring the proof to a setting where the companion hypotheses are not verified']
- **limit-analytic-entry-disk** (approximation): Analytic initialization holds only on a complex disk in the temperature whose radius shrinks with the observation side, for admissible sides beyond a threshold. The constants in this disk are not made explicit. Blocked use: ['Differentiating the entry activities in the temperature outside the disk', 'Deriving quantitative numerical thresholds from the disk radius']
- **limit-pinned-limits-positive-coefficient** (hypothesis): The pinned Gaussian limits assume Gaussian free limits with a positive coefficient and a fractional mean that becomes uniform and independent of the tests. Without these assumptions the pinned covariance statement is not available. Blocked use: ['Applying the pinned limit at scales where the profile value is zero', 'Dropping the uniform fractional mean assumption']
- **limit-fractional-mean-positivity** (hypothesis): Uniformity of the fractional mean is proved only when the profile value at the scale is strictly positive. Blocked use: ['Using the uniform fractional mean at scales where the profile vanishes']
- **limit-polynomial-decay-above-passage** (edge_case): The decay faster than any inverse power of the distance is established only at scales above the passage scale, along the extracted sequence, for distances in a fixed comparable range. Blocked use: ['Claiming superpolynomial spin decay at scales below the passage scale', 'Using the decay estimate uniformly over all distances without the comparability constraint']
- **limit-finite-size-criterion** (hypothesis): The finite-size mass bound requires the boundary correlation sum on the square box to be at most one half. The paper gives no finite procedure to evaluate that sum exactly. Blocked use: ['Certifying a mass lower bound from sampled or approximate boundary correlations without error control', 'Using the criterion on boxes that are not centered squares with free boundary']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Berezinskii–Kosterlitz–Thouless essential singularity for the correlation length of the nearest-neighbor cosine XY model on the square lattice. Let $`m(b)`$ be the mass obtained by taking first the free-box thermodynamic limit and then the separation limit along a coordinate axis. As the inverse temperature <i>b</i> approaches <i>b</i><sub><i>c</i></sub> from the massive side,

$`\displaystyle \sqrt{b_c-b}\log\frac{1}{m(b)} \longrightarrow A_{\mathrm{XY}},`$

where $`A_{\mathrm{XY}}`$ is a finite, strictly positive model-specific constant.
